## §14 · FAMILY M — sewers (surface works only): Sewage system (city L2225 "Underground drainage. Rare but critical for health.")

*Provenance: sub-lane LM. Evidence: Wikipedia Whittington's Longhouse; the Lewes Priory monks' toilet page; the 1189 Assize of Buildings (BHO, fetched — the one BHO page that passed the CAPTCHA); search digests on the Longhouse, the reredorters (Canterbury/Lewes/Kirkstall/Castle Acre), the 1189 Assize, the Cloaca Maxima and Frontinus; the Kirkstall reredorter page returned 18 bytes. DW law 7: the below-grade graph is the undercity train's; this family describes the SURFACE works — the latrine building, the cesspit, the reredorter, the outfall — and their joints.*

**(a) Analogue.** (1) **The public latrine**: Whittington's Longhouse, Cheapside ward (St Martin Vintry), opened "around 1 May 1421" with money left by Richard Whittington — "128 seats: 64 for men and 64 for women", "the first public toilet in the capital with separate provision for the sexes", built "on a long dock over the Thames" at the Walbrook's mouth (Bell Wharf Lane), "flushed by the Thames": "the waste was deposited in a gully which was washed by the tides twice a day"; ABOVE it "an almshouse for five or six parishioners"; destroyed in 1666 and "rebuilt on a more modest scale" with "six male and six female seats"; still leased in 1851; "longhouse became a byword for privy" (SUB-LANE CONFIRMED, [Wikipedia: Whittington's Longhouse](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Whittington%27s_Longhouse), fetched 2026-08-23, citing Sabine 1934 *Speculum*; CONFIRMED-digest for the ward/parish and the tidal gully). A 128-seat public latrine with an almshouse over it, on a dock, flushed by the tide — the only PURPOSE-BUILT sanitation building in the English medieval register this lane found, and it is a hospitality-poverty-utility building in one (families K and M share a roof). (2) **The monastic reredorter** (the institutional form): Lewes Priory's 11th-century block had "10 cubicles here but no doors", "built over a sewer. Chutes led down from each toilet to the sewer", "three large windows … provided light and ventilation", "probably a bath house at the west end"; "by the end of the 12th century a new, larger toilet block was built … to accommodate the growing number of monks", and "a longer dormitory was then built over the older toilet block" (SUB-LANE CONFIRMED, [Lewes Priory: Monks' Toilet](https://www.lewespriory.org.uk/monks-toilet), fetched 2026-08-23); the 12th-century Lewes block "at least 59 cubicles arranged over a drain channel"; Canterbury's "could accommodate 55 monks at one time"; Castle Acre "24 wooden-seated latrines in total with chutes running down into a stone lined channel which was fed with a supply of water from the nearby River Nar to flush the waste away"; Kirkstall's lay brothers' reredorter "linked to the dormitory and had a long row of cubicles arranged over the main drain" (CONFIRMED-digest, 2026-08-23 — no lengths in metres found). The reredorter is a LINEAR row of seats over a FLUSHED channel, attached to the dormitory at first-floor level, lit and ventilated by large windows — R-INST-3's monastic plan holds the building; this family holds the channel and the chute. (3) **The cesspit and the party-wall law** — the 1189 Assize of Buildings (Henry Fitz-Ailwin's): "if the pit made in such chamber be lined with stone, the mouth of the said pit shall be distant two feet and a half from the land of the neighbour, even though they have a common stone-wall between them. But if it shall not be lined with stone, it ought to be distant three feet and a half from the neighbour's land"; party walls "three feet in thickness and sixteen feet in height", each neighbour giving "one foot and a half of his land"; rain-gutters "between them, at their joint cost", or the water carried "into the King's highway" or "upon his own land"; "any kinds of pits made for receiving water, whether clean or foul" under the same procedure, judged "by view of such twelve men as were appointed" (SUB-LANE CONFIRMED, [BHO: Assize of buildings (Richard I)](https://www.british-history.ac.uk/no-series/london-mayors-sheriffs/1188-1274/pp179-187), fetched 2026-08-23; CONFIRMED-digest for the "necessary-chambers" wording and the 1189 date) — the cesspit is a CELL of the house (the "necessary-chamber" with its pit) under a distance law from the neighbour, and the Assize is the corpus's first written ADJACENCY PROHIBITION with numbers (0.76 m stone-lined / 1.07 m unlined). (4) **The Roman ceiling**: the Cloaca Maxima — "the gallery in the first section is 2.70 metres high and more than two metres large, progressively increasing until it reaches the outlet in the Tiber touching a maximum of 3.30 metres in height and almost 4.50 metres in width" (CONFIRMED-digest, 2026-08-23, of archeoroma / turismoroma); the Nîmes castellum's "three holes on the floor of the tank" drained to the sewers (family L); Milan's 1456 hospital corridors drained "directly into the underlying sewage system" (family J); Beaune's river under the hall (family J). **Structural imperatives**: WATER TO FLUSH (the tide twice a day; the Nar's channel; the Bouzaize under Beaune; "last for the removal of sewage" in Canterbury's use order — family L), FALL (chutes; the channel's gradient), SMELL/LIGHT (Lewes's three large windows), NEIGHBOUR (the 1189 distances), SEPARATION BY SEX (64/64; the Bristol workhouse spine — family K). **Siting**: the river or the tidal dock (the Longhouse; the reredorter over the leat at the dormitory's end — the monastic plan puts the latrine block DOWNSTREAM of the kitchen, R-INST-3), the cesspit in the yard or under the house against the party wall. **Live/work**: the gong farmer (PLAUSIBLE — searched by name in LM's query, not returned); the almsmen OVER the Longhouse. **Prosperity/wear**: floor (village) = a privy over a pit or a stream (NO_BUILDING — a fixture in the yard; the 1189 "necessary-chamber" is a CLOSET of the house); rung 1 (town) = the monastic reredorter over a flushed channel (10 → 24 → 55–59 seats) and the town's river-bank jakes (Exeter's leet complaints that "the jakes … is a great nosance unto the comens" — family L's LOCAL); rung 2 (city) = the 128-seat tidal public latrine with an almshouse over; ceiling = the Cloaca with a 3.3 × 4.5 m outfall and a castellum-fed flush. Decline: the Longhouse's 128 → 12 seats after 1666; the reredorter buried under a longer dormitory (Lewes) — the latrine is the first cell a rebuilding reuses as foundation.

**(b) Measured.** Longhouse 128 seats (64 + 64), 6 + 6 after 1666; almshouse for 5–6 above; 1421 (SUB-LANE CONFIRMED). Reredorters: 10 (Lewes 11th c.), 24 (Castle Acre), 55 (Canterbury), 59 (Lewes 12th c.) seats (digest). Assize 1189: 2½ ft / 3½ ft (0.76 / 1.07 m); party wall 3 ft × 16 ft (0.91 × 4.88 m); 1½ ft each (SUB-LANE CONFIRMED). Cloaca: 2.70 m high × > 2 m → 3.30 × 4.50 m at the outfall (digest). Frontinus's lacus count 591 (family L). NOT FOUND: any reredorter LENGTH in metres (the digest says so explicitly; Kirkstall's page was empty); the Longhouse's plan or dimensions (the Sabine article not fetched); a cesspit's measured depth/diameter; the gong farmer's rates; the covered town ditch (no search reached it). Seat PITCH is derivable: 128 seats on "a long dock" implies c. 0.6–0.75 m per seat in two rows of 64 — c. 40–48 m of bench (PLAUSIBLE arithmetic on a confirmed count).

**(c) Contested / counterexamples.** (i) The catalog's "Underground drainage. Rare but critical for health" is half right: underground MAINS are Roman (the Cloaca) and Milanese (1456) — rare indeed; but the medieval English register had SURFACE sanitation buildings at scale (128 seats) and flushed CHANNELS at every monastery — not rare, just not underground. The row's "rare" should be register-gated (underground = Roman/Italian; channel = monastic/English). (ii) The negation search for the 1189 distances was CONFIRMED from the primary — and the rule is a NEIGHBOUR law, not a health law: the Assize is about boundaries ("for the allaying of contentions between neighbours"), which is the correct mechanism for the grammar (a party-wall/lot law of DWR1A, not a sanitation pull). (iii) The reredorter's seat counts (10 → 59 at one house) track the community's size — capacity is the community's headcount, the same rule as the Frauenhaus (family F) and the almshouse (K): the cell is sized by PERSONS, not by prosperity. (iv) The Longhouse's almshouse-over-latrine contradicts any "latrine far from dwelling" adjacency one might assume — the tide, not distance, solved the smell; the grammar's `REQUIRED_FAR` for latrines must yield to a `flushed` attribute.

**(d) CIRCULATION typology (§452).** The reredorter: a `GALLERY`-shaped linear cell (a long row over a channel) entered from the dormitory's end at first-floor level (`REQUIRED_ABOVE(channel)`, `joint` from the dorter — R-INST-3's); the chutes are `VERTICAL{CHUTE}` joints (the CIRC addendum's latrine chute, now with Castle Acre's 24 and Lewes's 59); the channel is the undercity's (a law-7 edge under the building, with an inlet from the river and an outfall). The Longhouse: a two-row `GALLERY{RING?}`/double-loaded bench over a gully, with two entrances by sex (`duplicate(gender)` — family E's rule), and the almshouse stair above (a `STAIR_HALL` to the poor's rooms OVER a public latrine: the only `REQUIRED_ABOVE(latrine)` lodging in the corpus). The cesspit: a `CLOSET{NECESSARY_CHAMBER}` of the house with a pit under it and a DISTANCE joint to the neighbour's land (the first numeric `REQUIRED_FAR` in the program: 0.76/1.07 m). The Cloaca: a `CORRIDOR{SEWER}` 2.7 × 2 m rising to 3.3 × 4.5 m — walkable, the undercity's largest edge. Widths: the Cloaca's; the reredorter's NOT FOUND; seat pitch 0.6–0.75 m (derived).

**(e) STORAGE typology (§453).** The cesspit is a `STORE{FOUL}` — a pit, `lightReq NONE`, emptied by the gong farmer at night (PLAUSIBLE — not fetched), `REQUIRED_FAR(neighbour's land: 0.76–1.07 m)`, `FORBIDDEN_NEAR(well)` PLAUSIBLE (family L (c)); the `STORE{LIME/ASH}` beside a privy (PLAUSIBLE); the Longhouse's gully as a TIDAL store emptied twice a day (a store with a schedule — `cycle: TIDAL`); the reredorter's channel flushed by a leat (`cycle: CONTINUOUS`); the Cloaca's outfall. Prohibition: the 1189 distances; the Canterbury use-order (sewage LAST in the flow — family L (e)).

**(f) Typed proposal.** `parti: NO_BUILDING{PRIVY_PIT}` (village and below: a privy in the yard, a pit, or a stream — a fixture; the house's necessary-chamber is a `CLOSET` of DWR1A's parti with the 1189 distance law), `parti: REREDORTER_OVER_CHANNEL{seats 10–59; first floor off the dorter; chutes; flushed channel; windows}` (town: the monastic/institutional form — R-INST-3 owns the building, this family the channel and the count), `parti: PUBLIC_LONGHOUSE{seats 2 × 64 by sex; on a dock/over a tidal gully; almshouse over}` (city L2225 in the English register), `parti: CLOACA{2.7 × 2 → 3.3 × 4.5 m; castellum-fed}` (the Roman ceiling — the undercity train's node, with street-level drain mouths as the surface joints), `parti: HOSPITAL_CORRIDOR_SEWER` (Milan 1456 — family J's cell draining to a sewer). `functions[]`: floor = `pit`; rungs = + `seat + chute` → + `channel with water` → + `sex separation` → + `multiple seats over a flushed gully` → + `lodging over` → + `underground main`; ceiling = the Cloaca. `fixtures[]`: `SEAT{wooden}`, `CHUTE`, `GULLY{tidal}`, `CHANNEL{stone-lined}`, `LEAT_INLET`, `WINDOWS{large, for ventilation}`, `SCREEN{sex}`, `DRAIN_MOUTH` (street), `OUTFALL_ARCH{3.3 × 4.5 m}`. Structural buckets: `flushed{TIDAL|LEAT|NONE}`, `overWater`, `neighbourDistance{0.76|1.07 m}`, `ventilated`, `sexSplit`. Licensing: the privy every tier; the reredorter wherever R-INST-3's dorter is; the Longhouse city tier, era ≥ 1421, a BEQUEST (endowment — family K's `endowed` exception again); the Cloaca Roman. VERDICT: city Sewage system **NO_BUILDING (the graph) + BUILDING{PUBLIC_LONGHOUSE}** for its surface works in the English register; **UNDERCITY{CLOACA}** in the Roman; the village privy **NO_BUILDING** and the house's necessary-chamber a **CLOSET** of the dwelling. HOME: NO TYPED HOME for latrine, reredorter, cesspit, channel, outfall (the engine has no sanitation cell at all); FACET_INFERENCE `generic` (§Σ).

**(g) Consequences for the grammar.** (1) The FIRST numeric adjacency prohibition in the program comes from a primary law text (1189: 2½ ft stone-lined / 3½ ft unlined from the neighbour's land) — `REQUIRED_FAR` with a number and a material condition; DWR1A's party-wall law (3 ft × 16 ft, 1½ ft each) is in the same text, so the two programs share one source. (2) `flushed{TIDAL|LEAT}` overrides distance: a latrine may sit UNDER an almshouse if the tide carries the waste — adjacency laws need a `mitigatedBy` clause. (3) Capacity by community headcount (seats = monks; seats = 64 + 64 citizens) — the persons-not-prosperity sizing rule appears for the third time (F, K, M). (4) The sewage family is the cleanest statement of law 7's seam: every below-grade edge (channel, gully, cloaca) is the undercity's; every above-grade cell (seat row, closet, outfall arch) is the DW grammar's; the chute and the drain mouth are the typed joints. (5) A building with TWO institutions stacked (almshouse over latrine) — the `dualUse` relation (families H/I) in a vertical form: `REQUIRED_ABOVE` across institutions. (6) Catalog data note: "Underground drainage … Rare" is Roman; the English city row should carry the tidal Longhouse and the flushed channel as its measured forms.

**(h) Continental vs English.** England: the monastic reredorter over a leat (10–59 seats), the river-bank jakes, the cesspit under the 1189 distance rule, and one 128-seat tidal public latrine (1421); Rome: the Cloaca Maxima, a walkable main (2.7 → 3.3 m high) fed by the castellum's drains and the baths' overflow, with public foricae (PLAUSIBLE — not fetched); Italy: Milan's hospital sewer (1456) and Venice's canals as the sewer (PLAUSIBLE); the Low Countries' grachten and the German Ehgraben (the back-alley drain between house rows — PLAUSIBLE, not fetched) are the continental urban forms the English register lacks. The Islamic city's private cesspits emptied by a guild and the hammam's drains are the setting-agnostic cousins (PLAUSIBLE).

**(i) Fantasy note.** None — except that the undercity train's "fighting pit underground" (family I) and the sewer share a register in genre, and the Cloaca's walkable main is the historical ground for any "sewer level" the undercity draws.

## §15 · CONTINENTAL-VS-ENGLISH REGISTER — where this tranche is parochial and what the continental mechanism is

The per-family (h) paragraphs carry the detail; this section states the REGISTER RULE the grammar must carry for the tranche as a whole, in five findings.

**15.1 The heat engine is the culture gate, not the plan.** The drinking house (A), the inn (B) and the bathhouse (E) differ between England and the German lands by ONE fixture class: the open hearth in every drinking room (England) versus the single heated Stube with a Kachelofen (north of the Alps — "usually the only heated room, where all activities took place"), and the bath's stove-and-stones (Badstube) versus the hypocaust (Rome) versus the boiler-and-duct (hammam/Bañuelo). The partis converge (the courtyard inn; the narrow-deep drinking house; the thermal enfilade); the fixture set and the cell COUNT diverge (one Stube vs many hearths; one Schwitzbad vs a cold-warm-hot range). The grammar should gate the heat regime by culture and let the parti stand — the CIRC addendum's `heatRegime`-conditioned rules (§4.3 item 7) are this finding's home.

**15.2 Licence and jurisdiction are the English mechanism; ownership is the continental one.** England's tranche is a list of LICENSED functions on other people's buildings: the alehouse (1552), the tavern (1553 cap), the gaming room (1541 placard), the stew (the Bishop's liberty), the playhouse (the suburb "a few feet beyond" the lord mayor), the midwife (the bishop's licence), the post (the Exchequer's standing post). The continent's tranche is a list of OWNED buildings: the town-owned Frauenhaus, the state-run Ridotto, the Thurn-und-Taxis Posthalterei, the Fuggerei (a family's), the Innocenti (a guild's), the Ca' Granda (a duke's), the Venetian cistern (the Maggior Consiglio's). The grammar needs a `licence` attribute on FUNCTIONS (with a jurisdiction layer — the liberty) for the English register and an `ownedBy` attribute on BUILDINGS for the continental — and the culture weight (R-INST-2 §22) decides which dominates a settlement.

**15.3 Rome is the ceiling in six of thirteen families and the ORIGIN in four.** Mansio (C), thermae/fort bath (E), lupanar (F), theatre (H), amphitheatre + ludus (I), aqueduct + castellum + cloaca (L, M) are measured here from Roman primaries (Bearsden, Colosseum, Arles, Chester, Ludus Magnus, Nîmes, Vitruvius, the Cloaca) and the medieval forms are either revivals (Olimpico 1585; the bagnio 1679) or re-uses (Arles's 200 houses inside the arena; the Frangipani fortress). The Roman register is not "high fantasy" in this tranche — it is the MEASURED top of every utility and entertainment ladder, and the engine's `culture` axis must be able to draw it as a living register (the fantasy "colosseum" row is its honest use).

**15.4 The Mediterranean court versus the northern stack.** The osteria's "small internal courtyard", the corral de comedias in a tenement court, the Castelletto/Valence walled quarter with one door, the Innocenti's two cloisters, the hammam's domed sequence, the Venetian campo with its cistern — the southern register organises the tranche's functions round OPEN COURTS; the northern register stacks them (the narrow-deep alehouse "one room in width"; the two-storey Badstube; the Fuggerei's two-flat house; the four-apartments-per-stair St Cross lodgings). The inn is the one type BOTH registers build as a court (B) — which is why it is the tranche's spine.

**15.5 What the English register lacks and the continent supplies.** No public bath after 1546 (E); no purpose-built brothel, ever (F — only Rome's); no dedicated gaming house before the club (G — Venice 1638); no opera house before 1656 (H — Venice 1637); no ludus or arena (I); no aqueduct (L — "Exeter had no Pont du Gard"); no underground sewer main (M — the Cloaca; Milan 1456). What the English register supplies that the continent in this evidence does not: the polygon playhouse (H), the tidal 128-seat public latrine with an almshouse over it (M), the monastic conduit passages (L), the Assize of 1189's numeric neighbour law (M), and the endowed almshouse that has run 500–900 years (K). The grammar's `EUROPEAN_FANTASY_BASE` morphology should therefore treat "continental" as a UNION of registers (German/Swiss, French/Burgundian, Italian/Venetian, Iberian, Roman) each with a few signature partis, not as one alternative to England.

**15.6 Not researched (setting-agnostic cousins, all PLAUSIBLE and ledgered):** the han/menzilhane (C — R-INST-2 §16 holds the han), the Finnish/Russian smoke sauna (E), the Spanish posada and the Alpine Hospiz (B), the French relais de poste and tripot (C, G), the Spanish bullring and the Byzantine hippodrome (I), the Islamic bimaristan and the Byzantine xenon (J), the waqf imaret and the gerokomeion (K), the qanat and the su terazisi (L), the Ehgraben and the forica (M).

## §Σ · FINDING → DW-LAW MAP (charter §2 laws and §4 stages → the entries that ground them) and the ENGINE-GAP FLAGS

### §Σ.1 Law map

**Law 1 (functions, not rooms).** The tranche's floor is a FUNCTION on a host in nine of thirteen families: the alehouse on a dwelling (§2), the wayside inn on a roadside house (§3), the post-room on an inn (§4), the play on an inn yard or a guildhall and the hiring on the market ground (§5), the bath in a barber's Stube after 1560 (§6), the brothel on a licensed house (§7), the gaming table on a tavern room (§8), the prize on an inn yard (§10), the midwife on the patient's chamber (§11), the privy on the yard (§14). Prosperity buys walls in the documented order: the King's Head's hall → parlours → "new building" (§3); Chichester's open hall → two-room houses with chimneys (§11 — walls and heat arrive TOGETHER, and in reverse as decline).

**Law 2 (three-clamp ceiling).** Measured economic anchors: Southampton 1603 — 69 drinking houses for 4,200 people (1:61); 23 licensed alehouses a year 1619–24; three taverns by statute (§2); Hare's 10–20 / 2–5 / 1 inns by town rank (§3); Devon 1577 — 120 inns, 40 taverns, 400 alehouses (§3); 1686 — 179 beds and 287 horses in Southampton (§3); Rome's lacus every 900 persons (§13). Two EXCEPTIONS the clamp must express: ENDOWMENT (the almshouse and the Longhouse are funded outside the settlement's economy — §12, §14) and the WEALTH FLOOR on the builder (the Senate's 400,000 HS after Fidenae — §10).

**Law 3 (the floor is existence).** The REQ rows: "Water source" is NO_BUILDING at the floor (a spring or a curbed well) and a 1.1-m vault at the first rung (§13); "Inn (multiple)" and "Inns and taverns (district)" are buildings (§3); "Taverns (5–20)" is a COUNT SPLIT by licence class (§2). Law 3 therefore needs `NO_BUILDING` (R-INST-2 gap 1 — confirmed for 14 of 40 entries here, see §Σ.3) AND a RATIO-count REQ (alehouses per head; taverns per statute).

**Law 4 (derive, don't store).** No conflict, and three composition rules the tranche forces. (1) **The institution is a LICENCE with a date, and the licence is a circumstance, never stored building state**: the alehouse recognizance (1552), the tavern's statutory cap (1553), the gaming placard (1541), the bishop's midwifery licence, the stew's screen gate (1543) → lock (1557) → closure (1562), the bath's suppression (1546), the 1594 London ban on inn-yard playing, the postmaster's warrant. Each is a dated switch the derivation READS; a building re-derived at 1450 and at 1600 must come out differently without either plan having been persisted. (2) **A HOSTED institution derives through a host that itself derives** — fourteen of forty entries (§Σ.3) resolve into a keeper's dwelling, a patient's chamber, an inn's great chamber or a palazzo wing, so the pure function is of `(worldSeed, HOST buildingId, circumstances)` and the STABLE KEY belongs to the host, not to the institution: the alehouse's drink-room must land in the same cell of the same house on every derivation, or law 5's renovation reading breaks one level down. This is the tranche's addition to law 4 and the earlier tranches did not force it. (3) **`reclassedTo[]` is a derivation over the date, not a stored state machine** — bath → brothel / tannery / laundry → dwelling; arena → fortress → 200 houses → chapels → quarry; hospital → partitioned almshouse; conduit ownership monastic → civic → private: the fossil chain must fall out of the era input, and the arena's 1,500-year list is the stress case. One GAP, not a conflict: four joints in this tranche vary WITHIN a day — the stew's timetabled lock, the Fuggerei's 22:00 gates, the Longhouse's tide, the Hope's trestle stage — so `circumstancesSnapshot` needs a time-of-day (and tide) member or these joints are underivable (§Σ.2 item 6). DM edits stay the only persisted layer, and the tranche's named-person data (the almsman on the founder's roll, the innkeeper on the recognizance) is exactly the delta channel's business.

**Law 5 (stable anchors / renovation).** The tranche's dated sequences: merchant court → inn (Marshall's 1380 → 1406, §3); horse entry → coach by-pass → lobby; yard → roofed hall (Star, late 18th c., §4); open gallery → glazed → walled (§3); inn yard → playhouse (1598–99, §9); cockpit → playhouse (1616, 1629, §8); thatch → tile (1613, §9); bath → brothel / tannery / laundry → dwelling / barber only (§6); brothel: screen gate 1543 → lock 1557 → closed 1562 (§7); hospital → partitioned almshouse (1680; 1548/1570, §11); arena → fortress → 200 houses → chapels → quarry (§10); sugar house → mint → workhouse (1698, §12); conduit: monastic → shared → civic (1424) → private pipes (1698) → railway (1857, §13); reredorter buried under a longer dorter (§14). The ENDOWED building is the zero-renovation anchor (§12 — four almshouses still running).

**Law 6 (frontage from the parcel).** The inn's street range holds the gate, the parlours and the worst chamber (over the gate); the hall, kitchen and stables go BACK along the yard (King's Head; New Inn — §3); the alehouse's drinking room is the FORE chamber "next the street" and the keeper's bed is in it (§2); the playhouse has ONE door and the gatherer (§9); the Posthalterei puts the coach gate on the FACADE with a gable over it (§4); the Innocenti's loggia is the street (§12); the Fuggerei is an enclave with five gates (§12). Two frontage kinds the engine lacks: a GATE frontage (the carriage way as the street face) and a LIBERTY boundary (Bankside; the suburb; the enclave — §7, §9, §12).

**Law 7 (basements are the undercity's projection).** Joints this tranche adds: the cellar tavern with its own street steps (§2); the inn's two cellars as tap-room (§3); the Rose's sub-stage trap (§9); the hypogeum's 80 lifts and the Ludus–Colosseum tunnel (§10); the Innocenti's cryptoporticus (§12); the Venetian cistern under the campo and Exeter's conduit passages with manholes (§13); the reredorter chute, the cesspit, the tidal gully and the Cloaca (§14). The catalog's "Fighting pits … Underground" has NO surface-history ground (§10) and is the undercity's alone.

**Law 8 (the parti draw).** The measured parti catalog this tranche delivers (§Σ.3 lists them per entry): DWELLING_ALEHOUSE / SHOP_HOUSE_TAVERN / CELLAR_TAVERN / COURTYARD_TAVERN (§2); HOUSE_WITH_SPARE_CHAMBER / L_PLAN_GATE_AND_BACK_RANGE / COURTYARD_ONE_GALLERY / COURTYARD_GALLERY_RING / GATEHOUSE_BLOCK / DOUBLE_COURT / INN_STRIP (§3); COACHING_INN (= re-cut) / POSTHALTEREI (§4); BADSTUBE_HOUSE / ROMAN_RANGE / HAMMAM_DOMED (§6); LICENSED_STUBE_HOUSE / STEW_ROW / WALLED_QUARTER_ONE_DOOR / LUPANAR_CORRIDOR_CELLS (§7); COCKPIT_RING / GAMING_SHED_LINEAR / RIDOTTO_WING (§8); INN_YARD_PLAYHOUSE / POLYGON_YARD_GALLERIES / SQUARE_YARD_GALLERIES / HALL_PLAYHOUSE / COCKPIT_PLAYHOUSE / CORRAL / ITALIAN_BOX_TIER / ROMAN_CAVEA (§9); AMPHITHEATRE_ELLIPSE / LUDUS_COURT / SCAFFOLD_RING (§10); CHAPEL_WITH_BEDS / INFIRMARY_HALL_CHAPEL_END / CROCIERA / CELL_CLUSTER_ROUND_CHAPEL (§11); GATE_SPITAL / CLOISTERED_COURT_UNITS / STAIR_MODULE_LODGINGS / HALL_WITH_CUBICLES / WALLED_ROW_TOWN / FOUNDLING_LOGGIA / REUSED_HOUSE_WORKHOUSE (§12); WELL_HOUSE / CONDUIT_SYSTEM / CIVIC_CONDUIT / AQUEDUCT_CASTELLUM (§13); REREDORTER_OVER_CHANNEL / PUBLIC_LONGHOUSE / CLOACA (§14). Culture weight decides existence (§15.2, §15.5); the inn yard is the ANCESTOR parti of the playhouse and the bear garden (§5, §9, §10) — one `COURTYARD_GALLERY_RING` primitive with a stage fixture.

**Law 9 (vertical honesty).** Descending storey heights (12/11/9 ft) and the worst bed over the gate invert the status ladder (§3, §9 — already in §1.4); the Badstube's 4-m process ground floor under a dwelling (§6); the gallery floor 1.0–1.24 m above a raked yard (§9); the almshouse over the latrine (§14); the Fuggerei's one-flat-per-storey stack (§12); the external stair turret as a dated massing part (c. 1610, §9); the hypocaust basement and the hayloft as non-human storeys (§6, §3).

**S6 circulation (ODQ §452) — the tranche's typology in one paragraph.** `THROUGH_ROOM` is the floor everywhere and has THREE specialised forms here: the THERMAL enfilade (bath: cold → warm → hot with stepped, drained thresholds — §6), the SIGHTLINE hall (every bed sees the altar — §11) and the AISLED hall whose central aisle is a post-made corridor (§11). `CROSS_PASSAGE{ENTRY}` is the inn's gate passage with an ERA-GRADED width class — HORSE (medieval) vs COACH (≥ c. 1650; mail coach 1.8 × 2.45 m) — and a second passage in series to the stable court (§3, §4); the Bristol workhouse's sex-splitting `SPINE` (§12). `CORRIDOR` is licensed by PROGRAM four times with dates: the Lupanar's cell corridor (Roman, §7), Milan's latrine corridors (1456, §11), Exeter's conduit passages (1248–59 onward, 1.57 m high × one person — §13), the Cloaca (§14); and MINTED by partition once (Chichester 1680 — the nave becomes a spine between cells, §11). `GALLERY` is the tranche's richest class again: the inn gallery (Marshall's "alure" 1380 → the Star's unglazed → glazed → the New Inn's four-sided ring with an external stair; Pantin's "comparatively early" — §3), the playhouse's three-storey ring of 3.2–3.8 m depth (§9), the opera box tier of 0.95–1.2 m cells (§9), the cockpit/bear-garden tiers (§8, §10), the cloister walk of Ewelme (§12), the Innocenti's 71-m arcade (§12), the castellum's 1.5-m walkway (§13), the reredorter row (§14). `LOBBY/VESTIBULE`: the gatehouse chamber over the inn entry (§3), the playhouse's single door with the gatherer and the Hope's 56 × 16 ft gate range (§9), the brothel's lane gate — screened (1543), locked by timetable (1557) (§7), the Fuggerei's five 22:00 gates (§12), the Beaufort Tower (§12), the bath's Diele (§6). `STAIR_HALL`: winder → the inn's external flight to the gallery → Marshall's pair of stair turrets (1380) → Glastonbury's vice → the George's dogleg (c. 1730); the playhouse's external stair turret (≥ c. 1610); St Cross's four-apartments-per-door MODULE (c. 1445 — the CIVILIAN ancestor of the barracks module); Ewelme's ladder inside an endowed unit; the cellar tavern's street steps; the Lupanar's separate upper entrance (§2, §3, §7, §9, §12). `VERTICAL` (CIRC addendum): the well shaft and standpipe, the manhole with footholes, the reredorter chute, the hypogeum's 80 lifts, the heavens' winch, the cistern's canna (§13, §14, §10, §9). `EXTERIOR_WALK`: the inn yard (×2 at the New Inn), the playhouse yard (raked 5°), the market ground on statute day, the prize ring, the Fuggerei's streets, the arena (§3, §9, §5, §10, §12). NEW attributes forced: a `schedule` on severable joints (the brothel's timed lock; the Fuggerei's curfew; the Longhouse's tide), a `sequence` on enfilades (thermal; flow order drinking → washing → sewage), a `visibility` effect (the 1543 screen gate; the bed-to-altar sightline), a `MICRO` width bucket (< 1.2 m: opera box, conduit passage, well house), `dualUse` by timetable (the Hope; the pipe chamber as gun position), `joint.mandatoryOpen` (the London cellar door), `joint: OBJECT_ONLY` (the foundling wheel), and `reclassedTo[]` as a LIST (the arena's 1,500-year chain).

**S6 storage (ODQ §453) — the tranche's typology in one paragraph.** `CELLAR` is the PRIMARY cell twice (the cellar tavern; the inn's two cellars as tap-room) and separately-let once (Tackley's 1363) — §2, §3; `BUTTERY`/`PANTRY`/`LARDER`/`SPENCE` as the inn's medieval service pair-plus (King's Head 1613: spence beside the hall, larder beyond the kitchen — the ANNEXED polarity) and the alehouse's pot-buttery (§2, §3); `STILL_ROOM` = the brewhouse outshut (copper, tuns, cool-backs) and the hospital apothicairerie (130 pots) (§2, §11); `SCULLERY`-class = the bath's Schürkammer/külhan (boiler + fuel + store in one cell) (§6); `STORE` in new sub-forms — `FUEL` with a REFUSED door to the hot room (§6), `COACH_HOUSE` (§4), `PROPERTY/TIRING` (§9), `ANIMALS` with a hoist (§10), `CISTERN` and `SETTLING_TANK` in a flow order (§13), `FOUL` (the cesspit under the 1189 distance law — 0.76/1.07 m, the program's first numeric prohibition) (§14), `ALMS` at the gate (§12), `RAW/PRODUCT` pair in the workhouse (§12); `CLOSET` = the post-room (ledger, bag, horn — §4), Henslowe's office (§9), the keeper's pledge store (§7), the audit room (§12), the sacristy/revestiaire (§11), the Fuggerei's spare Kammer (§12); `ATTIC/GARRET_STORE` = cocklofts, haylofts, drying lofts, the "room used to put the tobacco pipes in" (§3), the Fuggerei attic (§12); the publican's fore chamber as the LINEN store (§2). Adjacency laws recovered: fuel ↔ hot room FORBIDDEN_DOOR (§6); cesspit 2½/3½ ft from the neighbour (§14); latrine `REQUIRED_OVER(water)` and `mitigatedBy: TIDAL` (§14); hayloft `REQUIRED_ABOVE(stable)` (§3); master's lodging `GUARDIAN_ADJACENT(gate)` (§12); kitchen `REQUIRED_NEAR(hall, dole gate)` (§12); sewage LAST in the flow order (§13); the cellar door `mandatoryOpen` (§2).

### §Σ.2 ENGINE-GAP FLAGS (consolidated; each names the family sections that demand it)

Only `brothel` and `gambling` resolve to `vice` correctly. The table is at `src/domain/spatial/cohesionWeave.js`; the fix is a `hospitality`/`entertainment`/`utility` kind or explicit tokens, not a catalog edit.
2. **`NO_BUILDING` / HOSTED outcome** (R-INST-2 gap 1/5, confirmed): 14 of 40 entries are NO_BUILDING or HOSTED at every tier (§Σ.3), including two REQ rows (thorp/hamlet Water source) — and the HOST can be a DWELLING of the demography layer (the alehouse keeper's house, the patient's chamber, a palazzo wing): `hostedIn: DWELLING(householdId) | EVENT_TARGET` (§2, §8, §11).

6. **`schedule` on severable joints and `dualUse` by timetable** — the brothel's lock (1557), the Fuggerei's 22:00 gates, the Longhouse's tide, the Hope's trestle stage, the pipe chamber's gun position (§7, §12, §14, §9, §13).

10. **Capacity by HEADCOUNT** — horses (6–50), women (26 → 10–12), monks (10–59 seats), almsmen (13), coach passengers — a `capacity` on `hostedIn` and on cells, sized by persons not prosperity (§4, §7, §12, §14).

12. **RING parti primitive** with N sides and a bay module (5 m = a rod): cockpit 12 m → Hope 24 m → Rose 22 m → Globe 30 m → amphitheatre ellipse 87–189 m; galleries 2–3 drawn; yard rake 5° (§8, §9, §10).

## §L · THE OPEN-QUESTIONS LEDGER (final for this dossier — 46 numbered items, built by grep over §0–§Σ, not from memory)

Construction: every `NOT FOUND` / `NOT MEASURED` / `not stated` / `not fetched` / `not researched` / `403` / `404` / `Cloudflare` / `digest only` / `DISCREPANCY` / `NUMBER AUDIT` / `ledger` marker in `head.md`, `sec-1.md`, `sec-A.md`–`sec-M.md`, `sec-15.md` and `sec-sigma.md` was extracted by script (`RINST4-markers.py` → `RINST4-markers.txt`, 210 marker sentences) and folded into the buckets below, so the ledger is complete by construction over the authored body. P1c re-verifies before the grammar consumes any of it.

### §L.1 · The three grammar-load-bearing figures that stayed OPEN (items 1–3)

These are the tranche's headline debts: each is a WIDTH the parti draw wants and no lane could measure. All three are now NOT FOUND by **convergence**, not by a search cap — four lanes (AB, CD, parent round 1, both resumes) issued at least ten distinct queries against them in four different vocabularies, and the last two rounds returned nothing new.

1. **The inn's carriage-passage clear width and height** (families B, C; engine-gap 4). Searched as "carriage entrance width height feet archway" (AB), "inn archway height" (CD), three parent queries in round 1, and — this lane, 2026-08-23 — in the building-recording vocabulary the earlier rounds had not tried (`medieval inn "cart entry" OR "cartway" OR "wagon entry" passage width feet historic building recording galleried courtyard inn`), which returned only encyclopedic pages and one teaching module. The Historic England entry for the New Inn (1245714) WAS read through the browser pane by sub-lane CD and states the carriage way's position and its posts but **no clear width**. Consequence: the `CROSS_PASSAGE{ENTRY, HORSE|COACH}` buckets in §3(d)/§4(d) are DERIVED from the mail coach's measured 1.8 m width × 2.45 m height (Science Museum 1912-32) plus clearance, and are labelled as derived. **The grammar may not quote them as measurements.**
2. **Any medieval inn GALLERY width or depth** (family B). The George's Survey of London description (six bays per gallery, cantilever beams, Doric columns), the 1874 plan reference, the wikisource Matz 1918 text and the New Inn's "arcade of six bays" all describe the gallery and none dimensions it. The only measured gallery in the corpus is the **playhouse** gallery (Rose, 10 ft 6 in / 3.2 m internal depth — and see item 20, that figure is itself an inference). Consequence: §3(d)'s gallery bucket is by analogy to the Rose, flagged as such.
3. **St Mary's Hospital, Chichester — the CLEAR SPAN of the infirmary hall** (family J; parent §1.5). Everything else about the building is now sourced several ways; the span is not. Sources reached: the almshouse's own site (six bays, kingpost roof "over 40'"), a digest of the Sussex Express article ("25 metres wide … 58 metres long when first built … chapel at the east end is 14 metres long"), britishlistedbuildings 101026704 by curl (C14 hall and chapel under one roof, roof on massive posts), and — fetched by the first resume lane, 2026-08-23 — a Silver Travel Advisor page giving **190 ft original length, 81 ft surviving, a 40-ft roof, 6-ft side walls, 16 aisle windows** and no span. This lane fetched the VCH Sussex ii entry directly (`british-history.ac.uk/vch/sussex/vol2/pp100-102`, 2026-08-23) on the theory that the JK sub-lane's block had been BHO's *search* CAPTCHA rather than the page: the page loaded and contains **no architectural dimensions at all** — history, governance, regulations and finances only. The span remains NOT FOUND. The §11(c) NUMBER AUDIT stands as the resolution the grammar should use: 25 m is an OVERALL width under one roof (an aisled hall), not a clear timber span, because a 25-m clear timber span would exceed Tonnerre's 21.5 m stone hall and nearly double Beaune's 14 m.

### §L.2 · Discrepancies carried with both sides (items 4–9)

4. **Chichester's length**: "58 m / 190 ft when first built" and "81 ft surviving" cannot both be reconciled with six surviving bays of an eight-bay hall (which computes to about 142 ft) — carried as an unresolved conflict, not silently averaged.
5. **Chichester's roof height**: "over 40 ft" (the almshouse site and one further source) vs "30 ft" (one digest). Two-to-one for 40 ft; both recorded.
6. **Tonnerre's hall**: 88.5 × 21.5 m (96 m with the apse), 18 trusses, from the Bourgogne-Franche-Comté inventory dossier, vs a digest's "90 × 18 × 27 m high". Take the inventory.
7. **Verulamium's Roman theatre**: c. 2,000 seats (one page) vs 7,000 (a digest); the diameter was never fetched (Kenyon's 1935 *Archaeologia* report not reached). The DISCREPANCY forecloses using Verulamium as a capacity anchor.
8. **The Rose's gallery storeys**: three (the model, Kiechel c. 1585 "about three galleries over one another") vs two (Greenfield). Unresolved — §9 rules that the grammar treat polygon-playhouse storey count as a DRAW within 2–3 rather than a constant.
9. **The Cockpit-in-Court's plan**: octagonal vs round, contested between two digests for the same building. Inigo Jones's 1629 plan would settle it and was not fetched.

### §L.3 · Figures resting on a search digest only (items 10–20) — never opened, so never quotable as primary

10. Pompeii Lupanar: cells c. 1.4 × 2.7 m, masonry beds c. 1.8 m, 5 + 5 cells (§7).
11. Whitehall cockpit c. 40 ft / 12 m diameter (§8).
12. Real-tennis court c. 90 × 27 ft (27.4 × 8.2 m); Bordeaux 29.1 m (§8).
13. Cloaca Maxima 2.70 m high × > 2 m, widening to 3.30 × 4.50 m (§14).
14. Reredorter seat counts — Lewes 10 (11th c.) and 59 (12th c.), Castle Acre 24, Canterbury 55 (§14).
15. The Great Conduit's 90 mm pipe bore, 1245 (§13).
16. A Sheffield well at 12.5 m (§13).
17. Letocetum's first timber mansio court, c. 62 × 62 ft / 18.9 m (§4).
18. Ewelme's unit description — "eleven of the Units comprised a single upstairs and downstairs room linked probably by a recessed stair or ladder" — is a digest of VCH Oxon xviii / Ewelme charity pages; the VCH page itself was not opened (§12).
19. Florence's Onestà (1403) as the magistracy licensing brothels (§7).
20. The Rose's gallery "internal depth about 10 ft 6 ins" is **Gurr and Orrell's inference** from the outer and inner wall lines, not an excavated dimension — the corpus's most-used gallery figure is a reconstruction, and §9 says so. Treat as PLAUSIBLE-derived when the grammar consumes it.

### §L.4 · Searched and genuinely absent (items 21–34) — absence-after-search, each a finding

21. Any measured **Badstube** plan. German-language searches returned bathroom-renovation adverts; the Nuremberg Irrerstraße excavation page carries no dimensions; the Heidelberg (Arnold) PDF sits behind an Anubis challenge (§6).
22. Any **hammam** room dimension (Bursa, Skopje, Sarajevo searched); the Bañuelo's room sizes are absent from its page (§6).
23. Any plan or room dimension of a medieval **Frauenhaus or stew-house** — Pullwitt is a legal history; the Bankside houses survive as Stow's words and a 1542 plan, neither fetched with figures. Also absent: the Valence quarter's size beyond "as large as a small town", and the Castelletto's form (§7).
24. Any plan of a **dicing house or bowling alley**; the Cockpit-in-Court's seating tiers and pit diameter from a primary; the **Ridotto's room count** (§8).
25. Any stated **stage size for an inn-yard** performance; the Blankenburg bed count; the Bargate hall's size (§5).
26. Any **bear-garden ring dimension** — the Survey of London states outright that none is recorded; the Pompeii gladiators' cells; any prize-ring or booth size (§10).
27. Any **"fighting pit"** at all. This is a NEGATIVE finding the grammar should keep: no fetched source puts a fight below ground, so the catalog's "Fighting pits … Underground locations" has no surface-history ground and belongs to the undercity train alone (§10, law 7).
28. Any **cloister-walk width, unit size or quadrangle dimension at Ewelme**; any measured stair, spine or cloister width anywhere in family K — every width in §12(d) is a bucket by analogy (§12).
29. The **Ca' Granda's arm lengths**; the **Beaune hall's bay count**; **any bed-spacing figure** in any hospital; the **Milan corridor width**; the **Norwich central-aisle width** (§11).
30. The **Great Conduit house's** size; the **Exeter receiver's** size; any conduit-house plan other than the Convent Well; a **Venetian cistern's capacity**; the **Pompeii water-tower's height**; the castellum's lead-pipe bores beyond the "40 cm" openings (§13).
31. Any **reredorter length** in metres — the digest says explicitly that none is published; Kirkstall's page was empty. Also: the **Longhouse's plan or dimensions** (the Sabine article not fetched); a **cesspit's measured depth or diameter**; the gong farmer's rates; the covered town ditch, which no search reached (§14).
32. The **Swan's** dimensions (de Witt's sketch names no figure in the fetched text); the **Teatro Olimpico's cavea width**; **any playhouse stair width** — none survives (§9).
33. The **Kempten gateway's** dimensions (the German page says "no measurements"); the **Stollhofen Posthalterei plan** — the Gutmann article has no plan (§4).
34. Any measured figure for a **tavern drinking room or cellar-tavern** in Southampton — Brown states in terms that "no ground plans have been located for early modern establishments" (§2). This is the cleanest absence in the tranche: the drinking house is documented by inventory, never by plan.

### §L.5 · Sources blocked, gated or corrupted (items 35–40) — a P1c work list, not an absence

35. **Historic England is the tranche's biggest single hole.** Cloudflare returned 403 to WebFetch and a challenge page to curl for list entries **1026704** (Chichester, blocked three ways across two lanes), **1245714** (New Inn — blocked to the parent and to AB), **1433280**, **1003851** (Exeter passages — a 60-byte response), and the HE disability-history hospitals page. **The recovery route that worked was the browser pane**: sub-lane CD read 1245714 and 1378357 (the George) through it and those two entries are the source of the only measured medieval inn CHAMBER in the corpus (7.9 × 4.7–5.9 m). P1c should plan browser-pane reads for HE from the start rather than spending fetches on 403s.
36. **British History Online**: its `/search?query=` endpoint serves a CAPTCHA (hit by AB and JK, and recorded as "VCH Sussex ii blocked"), but **direct volume-page URLs work** — this lane proved it by fetching VCH Sussex ii pp100-102 successfully (item 3), and the sub-lanes fetched Survey of London vol. 22 and VCH Glos. vol. 4 without trouble. The correct reading is that BHO is reachable and only its search box is not.
37. **403 to WebFetch, recovered by curl**: the-past.com's Exeter feature (the LOCAL source of record for family L), britishlistedbuildings 101026704. **403 and not recovered**: Berghahn Journals (Sagui 2025, family A's London cellar-door law — carried as a digest), the Swiss HLS Tavernenrecht article (twice — the continental legal tiering of the inn is unsourced), pearl.plymouth.ac.uk, si.edu's stagecoach object, ancientmonuments.uk's Letocetum entry, the Sussex Express article (403 to JK and again to the first resume lane's curl).
38. **404 / empty / redirect-gated**: the Cambridge Eagle Ward recording-project PDF (twice — an error page saved), Wikipedia's "St Mary's Hospital, Chichester" (no such page), Caerleon Amphitheatre and two Verulamium theatre titles, itv-grabungen twice, romanaqueducts.info's Nîmes page, a Springer article, the Cambridge excerpt PDF, chichestercathedral.org.uk's buildings page, reconstructingtherose chapter 5.1 (the rest of the monograph was recovered by curl with `-k` — its TLS certificate had expired), romantheatre.co.uk, Kirkstall's reredorter page (empty).
39. **A source-integrity finding worth carrying**: the exploringsouthwark.co.uk Bankside-stews page fetched by sub-lane EFG had been **hijacked by casino spam**. The sub-lane's evidence from it was discarded and family F rests on Pullwitt and the Bankside ordinances instead. P1c should treat small heritage sites as needing a content sanity check, not only an HTTP check.
40. **Paywalled and used only as an abstract**: Hare 2013 (*Journal of Medieval History*), the source of the inns-per-town-rank ladder in §1.6 and §3. The ladder (1 / 2–5 / 10–20 inns by town rank) is CONFIRMED-digest and is load-bearing for law 2's clamp on family B. P1c should buy or borrow the article.

### §L.6 · Deliberately not covered, with the reason (items 41–43)

41. **Setting-agnostic cousins, ledgered by §15.6 and named per family**: the han / menzilhane (R-INST-2 §16 already holds the han), the Finnish and Russian smoke sauna, the Spanish posada, the Alpine Hospiz, the Italian albergo, the French relais de poste and the tripot, the Spanish bullring, the Byzantine hippodrome and the maydan, the Islamic bimaristan, the Byzantine xenon, the Roman valetudinarium, the waqf imaret, the Byzantine gerokomeion, the qanat, the Ottoman su terazisi, the German Ehgraben, the Roman forica, the Spanish corral de comedias beyond one encyclopedic page, and the Roman civic thermae (Bath, Caracalla — the tranche's one Roman bath is a fort bath at Bearsden, a MILITARY register). Reason: budget went to the measured English and German primaries; every one of these is a bounded possibility for `EUROPEAN_FANTASY_BASE`, none is a prevalence prior, and none is needed before the grammar's first draw.
42. **Handed to a sibling tranche, cited not restated**: the carceral half of the workhouse and the guildhall as a performance host (R-INST-1 §7 and family A); the bardic college's collegiate plan and the monastic dorter over the reredorter (R-INST-3); the han and the culture weight (R-INST-2); the construction imperatives of the inn (DWR1A, cited in §3); the whole below-grade graph — the Cloaca, the conduit mains, the arena hypogeum's tunnels, the cellar fight — to the UNDERCITY train under law 7, of which this dossier types only the SURFACE joints (manhole, tap face, chute, drain mouth, trap, cellar steps); the magical and fantasy conventions of every row to R-INST-5, with one flag left in §6(i) (a magically heated bath deletes the furnace cell, which deletes the refused joint and the fuel-hunger pull).
43. **Out of register by date**: the coffee house, the club, the lying-in hospital (≥ 1749), the purpose-built English coaching inn of the 1780s–1830s beyond what §4 needs for the by-pass joint, and the Victorian workhouse. The tranche is sub-century medieval-to-early-modern per the product scope.

### §L.7 · What would most change a conclusion (item 44)

44. Five findings are one source away from moving. **(a)** A measured medieval inn gateway would replace engine-gap 4's derived HORSE/COACH buckets with data and is the single most useful fetch P1c can make. **(b)** Any measured Badstube or Frauenhaus plan would move families E and F from "a licence with no plan" to a drawable parti — at present both rest on ordinances. **(c)** A documented dedicated gaming BUILDING before Venice 1638 would refute family G's central verdict that gaming is always hosted. **(d)** A documented underground fighting place would move catalog entry 29 from undercity-only to a surface institution. **(e)** A measured medieval ENGLISH aqueduct would break §15.5's register split (the claim "no aqueduct in England" is an absence claim resting on Exeter's own evidence that it "had no Pont du Gard").

### §L.8 · Weakest links in what is asserted (items 45–46)

45. **Method limits inside the dossier's own claims.** The FACET_INFERENCE finding (§Σ.2 item 1) is a simulation of the regex table over the forty entry NAMES ONLY — the live institution record's `type` and `category` fields were never exercised, so it is PLAUSIBLE-by-simulation and P1c should re-run it against real records before the engine change is scoped. The verdict table's 20 BUILDING / 9 HOSTED / 11 NO_BUILDING split counts a PRIMARY verdict per row where several rows carry two verdicts by register, so the split is a reading, not a census. Every width bucket in §3(d), §4(d), §12(d) and §14(d) is derived or by analogy; §Σ's parti catalog mixes measured partis (Rose, Bearsden, Colosseum, Fuggerei, Beaune) with partis known only from ordinances (STEW_ROW, GAMING_SHED_LINEAR, WALLED_QUARTER_ONE_DOOR) and the grammar charter should carry that distinction forward as a confidence field on the parti enum.
46. **Absence claims doing real work.** "No purpose-built medieval brothel, ever", "no underground fighting pit", "no dedicated gaming house before 1638", "no English aqueduct", "no public bath in England after 1546" and "no opera house before 1637" are each an absence after genuine search rather than a positive finding, and §15.5's register rule is built on six of them at once. They are the tranche's load-bearing negatives and the honest statement is that each survived the searches this lane could afford, not that each is settled. Separately, the exact **curl** counts in §M are a floor rather than an exact figure (see §M.1) — a small method debt, recorded so a successor does not read the number as precise.

**WebSearch refusals: ZERO, at every stage.** This is a difference from R-INST-3, whose sub-lanes exhausted the shared 200-search session cap at 06:14:48Z; this tranche's six sub-lanes were killed by the chair's §465 stop before any refusal, and both resume lanes probed the cap and found it live (resume 1 at 14:40:49Z; resume 2 at approximately 14:58Z). No finding in this dossier is limited by the search quota.

**WebFetch outcomes across all 136 calls:** 102 OK, 15 × 403, 9 × 404, 10 redirect-gated / empty / challenge-page. The 403s and 404s are itemised by URL in §L.5 and by lane in the appendix.

**The curl figure is a floor, not an exact count.** The extraction truncates each Bash command at 300 characters, so a `curl` that appears later in a compound command is invisible to the counter; 27 is what is provably visible. An earlier pass by the first resume lane over the same logs, counting differently, gave 33 — both numbers are reported rather than one being quietly preferred, and the discrepancy is ledgered (§L.8 item 46). What is not in doubt is that curl carried the tranche's four most important primaries: Brown 2007 (54 MB PDF), Pantin 1958, the Bearsden excavation volume, and the *Reconstructing the Rose* chapters (that site's TLS certificate had expired, so `curl -k`).

### §M.2 · The recovery route — killed sub-lanes, reconstructed from their transcripts

Six research sub-lanes were dispatched at 06:05Z with a shared brief (`RINST4-subagent-brief.md`: the per-family output shape (a)–(i) and the closed vocabularies) and were **killed by the chair at approximately 06:15Z** under ODQ §465, which cut the program's lane cap to two. **Not one wrote its report file.** Everything in §2–§14 was recovered afterwards, on the route this program has now used twice (the memory-recorded method, proven for the V5 bank, the UI charter and R-INST-3):

1. Each sub-lane's transcript survives at `~/.claude/projects/-Users-cstokes-Desktop-settlement-engine/695a70c5-.../subagents/agent-<id>.jsonl`, outliving any scratchpad purge.
2. `RINST4-merge/extract.py` walks those files and writes, per lane, a `-calls.tsv` (timestamp, tool, arguments), a `-results.txt` (every tool result verbatim, with the call that produced it), a `-results.txt.clean` (the same with C0 control bytes stripped — the EFG results carried 827 of them) and a `-notes.md` (the sub-lane's own assistant text and thinking).
3. The parent read the `.clean` results in full, family pair by family pair, and wrote `sec-A.md` … `sec-M.md` from them, marking every claim with the provenance tier it actually has.
4. `assemble.sh` rebuilds the dossier from `head.md` + `sec-1.md` + `sec-A..M` + `sec-15` + `sec-sigma` + `sec-ledger` + `sec-method` + `sec-appendix`. **The assembled file is never hand-edited**; a correction is made in a section and the dossier is regenerated.

The evidence tier `CONFIRMED (sub-lane)` — written in the sections as **SUB-LANE CONFIRMED** — means exactly this: a page fetched by a sub-agent of this lane on 2026-08-23, whose result text was recovered from that sub-lane's transcript and quoted with its URL. It is weaker than a parent fetch (the parent did not see the page render) and stronger than a digest (the page body was actually returned).

### §M.3 · Files on disk (the LOCAL tier)

`LOCAL` in the sections means a primary text saved to the scratchpad by a sub-lane and read directly by the parent, not summarised through a digest. Those files, verifiable on disk: `RINST4-AB-brown-2007-southampton.pdf/.txt` (James Brown's 2007 Southampton thesis, 806 KB of extracted text — the tranche's single richest source, carrying the alehouse/tavern/inn room counts), `RINST4-AB-pantin-clarendon-1958.pdf/.txt` (Pantin's Oxford inns study — the Star, the King's Head 1613 inventory, the gallery and the coach by-pass), `RINST4-EFG-bearsden-excavations.pdf/.txt` (Breeze & Banks, the one room-by-room measured bath in the tranche), `RINST4-EFG-pullwitt-frauenhaus.pdf/.txt` (the Nuremberg Frauenhaus law), `RINST4-HI-rose-*.txt` (twelve chapters of *Reconstructing the Rose*), `RINST4-JK-fox-brazier-midwives.pdf/.txt` (midwife licensing), `RINST4-LM-exeter-thepast.txt` and `RINST4-LM-subbrit-exeter.txt` (the Exeter conduit passages), `RINST4-CD-stollhofen-posthalterei.pdf/.txt` (Gutmann on the Thurn-und-Taxis post), `RINST4-CD-sol22-boroughhigh.txt` (Survey of London vol. 22), `RINST4-CD-gloucester-pubs-edmonds.pdf/.txt`, `RINST4-CD-HE-1245714-newinn.txt` (the New Inn list entry, read through the browser pane), `RINST4-AB-kyl-borough.txt`, `RINST4-JK-7739ec9b.html` (britishlistedbuildings, Chichester).

Five files on disk are the Cloudflare challenge page itself, each exactly **5,838 bytes**: `RINST4-AB-HE-1245714.html`, `RINST4-HI-he-hope-1433280.html`, `RINST4-JK-he-1026704.html`, `RINST4-LM-exeter-HE-1003851.html`, `RINST4-P-HE-1026704.html`. They are kept as evidence of the block, not as sources. Three more are empty or error artefacts: `RINST4-AB-plymouth-urban-inn.pdf` (0 bytes), `RINST4-LM-kirkstall-reredorter.txt` (18 bytes), `RINST4-EFG-arnold-badewesen.pdf` (4.8 KB — an Anubis challenge, not the article).

### §M.4 · Negation searches — issued per family, results reported in each (c)

The brief required each family to be attacked from its negation as well as its claim. The queries actually issued (verbatim strings in the appendix) include: "medieval alehouse 'no separate' building alewife brewed in her own house"; "'most villages' no inn … villages without inns"; "medieval English hospital 'no infirmary hall' OR 'never had an infirmary'"; "'almshouse' England before 1550 … 'no workhouse' pre-1600"; "Southwark stews suppressed 1546 … bathhouses closed syphilis"; "Unlawful Games Act 1541 'common house' dicing bowling alley"; "bare-knuckle prize fight venues … were fights held in cellars?"; "medieval English towns wells contaminated by cesspits; piped conduit water rare before 1600; Roman aqueducts abandoned medieval period counterexample". Four of them returned findings that **changed** a section rather than confirming it: the alehouse negation confirmed the dwelling verdict AND produced the twelve-room counterexample (§2(c)); the village-inn negation returned a structural answer rather than a null (§3(c) — because it was ANSWERED there rather than left open, it carries no ledger number); the piped-water negation was CONTESTED by Exeter's own four gravity systems (§13(c)); the cellar-fight negation produced the tranche's cleanest NEGATIVE finding — no fetched source puts a fight underground (§10(c), ledger item 27).

### §M.5 · What this lane (RESUME 2, Opus) did

Read the receipt whole, `head.md`, `sec-15.md` and `sec-sigma.md`, the R-INST-3 tail as the style template, and the marker sweep over all seventeen authored files. Found and fixed two defects the first resume lane left: **(a)** the §Σ law map ran 1, 2, 3, 5, 6, 7, 8, 9 — **law 4 (derive, don't store) had no paragraph**, and one is now written from the tranche's own evidence (the licence-as-circumstance rule, the derive-through-a-host rule, the `reclassedTo[]`-as-derivation rule, and the time-of-day gap); **(b)** in `head.md` §0.2 THE FAMILIES, the Members column of rows I, J and L had been clobbered by a `sed` that matched both tables, and the originals were restored from `dossier-checkpoint-0608Z.md`. Then wrote the ledger, this disclosure and the appendix, rewrote the banner and the status map, reassembled and scanned.

Two WebSearch and two WebFetch, all four aimed at the three OPEN figures on angles the earlier lanes had not tried, all four **null** — reported because a null after a genuinely new angle is a finding: (1) `medieval inn "cart entry" OR "cartway" OR "wagon entry" passage width feet historic building recording galleried courtyard inn` — the building-recording vocabulary rather than the "archway/carriage entrance" vocabulary of the earlier rounds; returned encyclopedic pages only. (2) `"St Mary's Hospital" Chichester infirmary hall roof span aisled "Sussex Archaeological Collections" OR Pevsner width of nave feet`. (3) WebFetch `https://www.british-history.ac.uk/vch/sussex/vol2/pp100-102` — testing the hypothesis that the JK sub-lane's "VCH Sussex ii blocked" was BHO's *search* CAPTCHA and not the page; the page loaded and has **no architectural dimensions at all**, which both closes the hypothesis and corrects the record (§L.5 item 36). (4) WebFetch `https://warwick.ac.uk/fac/arts/history/students/modules/hi390/topics/session11/plans/` — a teaching module with inn and tavern plans; the plans carry **no dimensional annotation**.

### §M.6 · Stopping rule — where each conclusion actually stopped

Three different stops, and the dossier should not be read as if they were one. **(a) The six sub-lanes were stopped by the CHAIR at approximately 06:15Z**, mid-investigation, none by convergence — everything in §2–§14 is what nine to ten minutes of fan-out reached, and the appendix prints exactly how far each got. **(b) The two resume lanes were merges, not investigations**, apart from ten web acts between them. **(c) The three headline OPEN figures (§L.1) stopped by CONVERGENCE**: four lanes, at least ten distinct queries in four vocabularies, two consecutive rounds returning nothing new. That third stop is the only one entitled to be read as "the source does not exist in reach"; the other two mean "the lane ran out of time".

### §M.7 · Engine recon (read-only, at `chair-baseproof-b10ed1a1`)

`src/data/institutionalCatalog.js` (2,526 lines, 311 entries; the six tier blocks and their line ranges are printed in §0), re-read at the line numbers of every candidate row; `RINST2-catalog-flat.tsv` re-read whole (311 rows) for the roster sweep; `src/domain/interior/interiorTemplates.js` (the `vice` template and the `heals` function variant); `src/domain/spatial/cohesionWeave.js` `FACET_INFERENCE` (the regex table, plus a node simulation of it over this tranche's 38 names — PLAUSIBLE-by-simulation, see §L.8 item 45); `docs/DESIGN_DWELLINGS_PROGRAM.md` §2 re-read for the nine laws that §Σ.1 maps, plus §3, §4 S6, §5, §8 and §15; ODQ §435–§438, §452, §453, §456, §462, §465. **No repo file was written, no test run, no git command that mutates state.**

Reading: the body is carried by 270 fetched-or-local tokens (124 sub-lane fetches + 123 LOCAL primaries + 16 parent fetches + 7 parent re-verifications) against 88 digest-only and 81 PLAUSIBLE ones. **No figure that §Σ hands to the grammar rests on a PLAUSIBLE label**, and every width bucket that is derived rather than measured says so in its own sentence as well as in the ledger. The tranche's honest weak spot is not the count but its distribution: families A, B, C, E, H, J, L are carried by LOCAL primaries, while F, G and the continental register of D, I, K, M lean on digests and ordinances (see §L.8 item 45).

Six research sub-lanes ran from 06:05:54Z and were killed by the chair at approximately 06:15Z under ODQ §465 (the lane cap cut to two), between three and ten minutes into their work. **Counts.** 61 tool calls (12 WebSearch, 16 WebFetch, 5 Bash calls invoking curl), 06:06:27 – 06:15:39Z — the **last lane standing**; its final act at 06:15:39Z is the last recorded act of the whole fan-out. Killed by the chair; no final report.
